# Acceptance stops

Open evidence gaps (see `docs/PLAN.md`, "Acceptance stops"). A stop blocks only
what depends on it.

`Kind` says how the stop is enforced:

- `project`: a provenance fact about the project's inputs. No code path can
  report it at runtime, so there is no test pin; the row (and the PLAN rule it
  cites) is the record.
- `code`: a gap inside the code. It must be reported explicitly at runtime
  (the "Reported by" column names the mechanism) and pinned by a real test
  (the "Pinned by" column), which asserts the report, never a print.

| ID | Kind | Area | Gap | Reported by | Pinned by | Blocks | Cheapest resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| S-011 | code | INI include paths | `Win32Path` (portable PathRemoveFileSpecA / PathAppendA / PathCanonicalizeA, fitted to the real shlwapi: random paths per call plus long ones, zero mismatches inside the domain) refuses forms it does not port: any length, component length or running length of 250-262 (shlwapi truncates and drops trailing separators there in unported ways), UNC `\\server\share`, `\\?\` device paths, doubled separators, and EMBEDDED DRIVE COMPONENTS, i.e. any `:` other than the `X:` prefix at index 1 (`.\C:\..\x.inc` from `parent.ini` is `C:\x.inc` in shlwapi but the port gave `\x.inc`; the generator now builds these and the fuzz audit shows `:` is the only special character that diverges). Retail data has no such include: the all-`*.ini` pre-pass resolves every include with zero errors. | `Win32Path::isSupportedPathForm()` is false, so `TextFile::resolveIncludePath` returns false and the include is a loud "Could not open include" error (lane INI-1) | `Win32Path refuses embedded drive components (S-011)`, `Win32Path refuses forms outside the ported domain`, `Win32Path reproduces the MAX_PATH failure state ...` and the shlwapi oracle test, whose generator builds colon and special-character segments and asserts they are refused (test_win32path.cpp) | mod or map INI includes with such paths | model the embedded-drive and truncation-band rules against the real calls, or call the real shlwapi on Windows builds |
| S-012 | code | Archive mounts | A mounted disk archive is protected from replacement only where the platform can deny other writers: `_WIN32` (MSVC and MinGW, `_wfsopen` with `_SH_DENYWR`). Elsewhere a rename-over is harmless (the open inode is untouched) but an in-place overwrite cannot be prevented, so a live mount would read the new bytes (`ArchiveHandle`, lane INI-1). An advisory `flock` would not close this: it binds only writers that also take the lock, and an arbitrary process overwriting the file does not. | `ArchiveFileSystem::unverifiedIdentity()` returns one `S-012: archive identity unverified ...` line per mounted archive whose handle is not replacement-protected (`ArchiveFile::isIdentityEnforced()`); empty on Windows | `the mount reports S-012 exactly when the platform cannot enforce deny-write` (asserts empty on `_WIN32`, an `S-012:` line elsewhere) and `an archive opened without deny-write is reported as S-012 on every platform` (test_mount_handles.cpp) | mount-identity guarantee on Linux/macOS builds when another process rewrites an archive in place | compare size and mtime (or a content hash) on every read and fail loudly, or copy archives into private storage at mount |

| S-016 | code | Retail oracle (Apt strings) | Retail string formatting allocates through the game's pool allocator (BFME2 0x00ADB160, a global that startup builds; the helper does not). So the harness cannot run (a) Add2 with a string operand (concatenation) and (b) Equals2 of a non-string, non-boolean value against a string, whose getName compare formats the other side; both fault at BFME2 0x00ADB177. The engine's behaviour for these cases has only hand-traced expected values. | `AptHandlers.run` raises `OracleError` at 0xADB177; the comparison tests EXCLUDE exactly those Equals2 cases and assert the exact exclusion counts (`EXCLUDED_EQUALS2`) | `test_stop_s016_string_concatenation_and_string_vs_nonstring_equals2_fault_in_retail` (the faulting Add2 and Equals2 cases, and that the neighbouring supported cases run) and the exact counts asserted by `test_binary_handler_matches_retail_on_edge_and_random_operands[equals2-6/7]` (tools/retail_oracle/test_apt_handlers.py) | claiming external verification of string concatenation and of Equals2 string-vs-number getName compares | build the pool allocator state (the object behind the global read at 0xADB167) in the helper, or hostfn-backed allocator hooks as `prime_asciistring_runtime` does for RW |
| S-017 | code | Apt atof | Retail MSVCR71 atof differs from correct rounding for decimal texts whose exact value lies in [2e-324, DBL_MIN): it returns TWICE the value for exact powers of two there (and 0 for 2^-1023), the exact decimal of 2^-1075 is 0x1 in retail but 0 in the engine (Boolean true vs false), and 2.225073858507201259573821257020768020077017763406988739288377e-308 is 0x000FFFFFFFFFFFFF in retail but 0x0010000000000000 in the engine. The cause inside the CRT is not decoded. | `AptAtofDouble` decides from the exact value of the TEXT (digit comparison against DBL_MIN's exact decimal expansion, floor 2e-324), so it reports even when the engine's rounded result is zero or a normal number: a pending report "... subnormal double range ... | S-110 | code | Map object initial state | A map object's Drawable starts with model condition flags NIGHT / SNOW only (the map's time of day and weather through GameData `ForceModelsToFollowTimeOfDay` / `ForceModelsToFollowWeather`, and the `objectTime` / `objectWeather` keys, RW 0x695EBD-0x695F2F). Everything else object creation sets is not derived: weapon set and veterancy flags, the damage state from `objectInitialHealth` / `objectMaxHPs`, upgrade visuals (`objectUpgradesList`, `objectGrantUpgrade*`), power (`objectPowered`), stance, construction state, `BuildVariations` (a logic RNG pick), the owner colour is the SidesList `playerColor`, not an MP lobby colour, and the Lua `OnCreated` creation hooks: retail sends every full object (and horde member) the event `OnCreated`, and the handler of its AI module's `AILuaEventsList` (`data\scripts\scriptevents.xml` `EventList` / `Inherit` / `EventHandler EventName="OnCreated"`, function bodies in `data\scripts\scripts.lua`) hides sub objects permanently (`RangerFunctions` -> `OnGondorArcherCreated` hides `FireArowTip`, which `SubObjectsUpgrade` shows again when the fire arrow upgrade is granted, so archers draw their fire arrow tips; also `Forged_Blade`, `Glow`, `Hammer1`, `Torch` ...) or grants upgrades. `MapObjectRuntime::build` RUNS them on the real Lua script engine (lane LUA-1): after the object's draw modules exist the object enters the script world (`LuaScriptEngine::objectEnteredWorld`, RW 0x68E31F) and receives `OnCreated` (`dispatchInternal`; RW 0x628882 / 0x6288FA sendObjectCreated binds the Drawable first, then dispatches), and a hide reaches the drawable through `LuaGameHost::drawableShowSubObject` / `drawableShowModule` (a draw module of that tag first, RW 0x6789B4: the matched module takes the request, its placed model is marked `moduleHidden` and not instanced; a matched module that shows no model here is the reported S-124). What runs is decided by the real event registry from the raw `scriptevents.xml` / `scripts.lua` (`CreationScriptData::rawLoaded`), never by the report-only inventory scanner: a one-line function, a redefinition, a handler assigned through a global expression or a computed hide argument run, a handler naming a missing global is retail's silent failure (RW 0x736019), and a scanner failure is only the `[S-110]` line `the report-only inventory scan ... failed`. Inheritance is resolved from the binary: the handler insert (RW 0x733FAF) overwrites an existing handler of the same event, so a child list's `OnCreated` REPLACES the inherited one (17,790 objects ran 35 own handlers; `OnMordorFighterCreated` 2,274, `OnIsengardFighterCreated` 2,866). Still stops: the order register-then-OnCreated is inferred (spec G7); the logic RNG draw `GetGameLogicRandomValue(1, 999)` (GameLogic.cpp line 0x19A7, RW 0x628892..0x6288A5, unconditional) IS made, in `LuaScriptEngine::sendObjectCreated` (the one shared creation function: draw, then the drawable, then register + OnCreated) for every full object, bridge and horde member, hook or not (51,414 draws over the 181 maps; `MapRuntimeReport::creationDraws`; the generator is the one `MapObjectRuntime::setLogicRandom` names, its algorithm and the seed at map start stay S-080, and the drawing order is the object list order, not retail's earlier bridge / wall pass (IsBridge or WALK_ON_TOP_OF_WALL objects are created first): a bridge / wall pass object that follows a normal object makes `MapObjectRuntime::build` report `[S-110] N bridge / wall pass object(s) ... follow a normal object ...` (`MapRuntimeReport::creationOrderDeviations`), because every later logic draw of the map can then differ from retail); `ObjectGrantUpgrade` / `ObjectHasUpgrade` / `ObjectDoSpecialPower` ... are engine callees that do not exist (S-124: `findUpgrade` and the others are reported, the upgrade is not granted, so e.g. `OnTrollCreated`'s rock-throwing upgrade is missing); a template with several AI modules naming lists uses the first; and a script's hide / show of a name no sub object has (7 names, `arrow`, `arrownock`, `icewall`, `moduletag_drawfloor`, `spear`, `v1s`, `v2s`) is ignored as ZH `doHideShowSubObjs` does (the INI's own 7 names `arrow`, `arrownock`, `icewall`, `moduletag_drawfloor`, `spear`, `v1s`, `v2s`, plus every name a hook hides that some model lacks, e.g. `FireArowTip` on 2,554 of the 6,921 archers' hides, `Forged_Blade`, `Glow`; the name `ModuleTag_DrawFloor` is a draw module tag: no retail object has such a floor hidden at map start). | `MapObjectCreation::stopLines()` `[S-110]` in `MapObjectReport::stops` (carried in every `MapObjectBuilder` report) and `MapObjectReport::unportedKeys` (which keys the objects carry) and the creation hook report (`creationHooksScanned`, `creationHookObjects`, `creationHooksByFunction` / `ByTemplate`, `creationHookHides` / `Shows`, in the `MapObjectBuilder` report `objects.creation_hook*`) | `mapobj creation: initial model condition flags ...` and `mapobj creation: every stop line is registered ...` (test_mapobj_creation.cpp); `mapobj retail: the object loop ...` pins the complete `unportedKeys` table and the complete creation hook tables (test_mapobj_retail.cpp); `mapobj creation hooks: ...` (test_mapobj_creation.cpp: the scans and the report on synthetic data), `mapobj runtime: scripts the report-only scanner cannot read still run on the real engine ...`, `mapobj runtime: every full object draws GetGameLogicRandomValue(1, 999, GameLogic.cpp, 0x19A7) before its drawable and OnCreated ...` (call log with and without hooks and a GetRandomNumber handler), `mapobj runtime: a hide of a matched draw module that shows no model here is reported S-124 ...`, `mapobj runtime: a bridge / wall pass object after a normal object keeps drawable-list order ...` (call-log sequence and the exact S-110 report); | S-1121 | code | Disconnect path (MP-2) | RotWK's DisconnectManager is ported (update RW 0x8D8A90, updateDisconnectStatus RW 0x8D8735, votes RW 0x8D7F77 / 0x8D8233 / 0x8D826F, processDisconnectFrame RW 0x8D836B with sendFrameDataToPlayer RW 0x8D4761, allOnSameFrame RW 0x8D861A, isLocalPlayerNextPacketRouter RW 0x8D86D0, disconnectPlayer RW 0x8D8553 / 0x8D57D8, the GameData network fields RW 0xC007D0 .. 0xC00850 with the constructor defaults RW 0x6432FF ..). Not located / not ported: the sender of DISCONNECTSCREENOFF (type 29 is inferred from the gap in RW 0x989449; ZH's notifyOthersOfNewFrame on screen off is kept), the GameSpy ping test RW 0x8D7C78 (no pinger in a LAN game: it passes), TheGameLogic + 0x2A4, the left state 1 of ConnectionManager + 0x12080, the filler of the router fallback order (+ 0x12030: here the network slots in slot order), RotWK's frame gate (ZH's screen gate would deadlock a peer that is behind: not applied), the disconnect chat. OpenBFME addition: a peer that announced its disconnect frame F runs F only after a DISCONNECTPLAYER, a later frame of an in-game peer, or the next packet router's release (a DISCONNECTSCREENOFF that lists the announcement), so a drop at F and the late arrival of the leaver's commands for F cannot both happen; two peers that disagree on who the next router is can still race (two failures at once). Inferred (lane MP-2 review): the decider of a drop is the first slot of the lobby's fallback order whose predecessors are all the target or gone, and its DISCONNECTPLAYER names the predecessors it holds gone (a slot mask, OpenBFME's field): a receiver refuses it at once when a predecessor that is not the target is unnamed (judged on the message and the agreed order alone), holds it while a named leave / drop has not been applied here (the frames from the drop frame on wait), and applies it before the drop frame runs, else refuses it (Network::dropClaims / verifyDropClaims); so a membership change never depends on which messages arrived first. With two or more silent slots ahead of every survivor in that order no survivor may decide: the disconnect screen stays until the players Quit | `DisconnectManager::stopLines()` `[S-1121]` (every peer report) | `mp2 disconnect: the lane's acceptance stops are reported (S-1121 .. S-1126)` and the other `mp2 disconnect:` tests (test_mp2_disconnect.cpp) | retail-exact disconnect timing in corner cases, cross-play | read RW 0x8D4E89 / 0x8D2F04 (the command dispatch) for type 29 and the writers of ConnectionManager + 0x12080 / + 0x12030 |

| S-1140 | code | Presentation beyond retail: horde motion (SMOOTH-2) | By the owner's direction (2026-10-06: the infantry and horse battalions "jerked around a lot and seemed to 'lag'") the drawn position is not retail's: RW 0x6765B9's D3DXVec3CatmullRom takes P3 = the pending position, which for a member walking to its slot without a path is the pre-move position (RW 0x5E5B4E), so retail's curve slows to a stop at the end of every logic frame (the drawn speed jumps about 3x at each boundary at 144 fps). `RenderInterpolation::smoothPose` (drawn by `Drawable::syncFromSnapshot`) keeps retail's stale snap and rotation, uses the pending position only as a look-ahead (set in the frame and not the frame's start position), otherwise extrapolates the step, holds a segment without motion, and limits the Hermite tangents (no backward part along the step, at most twice its length). The presented time (`LiveGame::present`) moves continuously, eased towards the clock by at most a quarter of the render step, stops at the end of the latest completed frame while the worker is late, and each late render frame adds 4 ms to the presentation delay (at most +70 ms, decaying 10 ms per second). Presentation only: the simulation and its hash are untouched. Projectiles keep retail's pose. | `[S-1140]` in `DrawableManager::Report::stops` (`RenderInterpolation::stopLines()`) | `smooth2: smoothPose: even steps are linear, a stop holds ...`, `smooth2 retail: hordes and cavalry march, turn, charge ...`, `smooth2 retail: a worker slower than the presentation delay never freezes and jumps ...` (test_smooth2_horde_motion.cpp), `smooth1: the smooth-motion stops are reported ...` (test_smooth_interpolation.cpp) | how close the motion looks to retail at 30 fps | none needed for play; a retail capture of a marching horde at 30 fps shows the pulse this removes |

| S-123 | code | Lua numerics | Script arithmetic (`+ - * /` and the `for` step) is rounded to a 24 bit significand like retail's x87 at precision control 24 (setFPMode RW 0x440809; one rounding of the exact result, checked against the hardware-verified NumericState). That the game runs at PC24 while scripts execute is assumed, not traced; `LUA_EA_NUM_DOUBLE` selects plain doubles. Number text is MSVCR71's %.16g (3 digit exponents, 1.#INF, 1.#QNAN, -1.#IND: CRT facts, the binary only holds the format string RW 0xD0AC04), produced from the exact decimal expansion of the double by an own formatter (no host snprintf / localeconv, independent of the FPU rounding mode and the locale) with MSVCR71's own TWO STAGE rounding (17 digits then 16, each rounded up by its guard digit alone, read from the DLL's instructions `_fltout` 0x7C372A35 / `$I10_OUTPUT` 0x7C37203C / 0x7C355AA0 and run in an x86 emulator; not the correctly rounded text: 1000000000000002.5 prints 1000000000000003, either zero prints `0`; 72,590 sampled doubles agree) and strtod is MSVCR71's (no inf, nan, hex; subnormal results are the host's, S-017 shows MSVCR71 mishandles them); `tonumber(s, base)` is a fixed width 32 bit unsigned strtoul (sign negated mod 2^32, overflow 0xFFFFFFFF, RW 0xB5FA89..0xB5FAE1) and the luaL_check_int/long conversions are `_ftol` (low 32 bits). UNRESOLVED: no running Windows oracle (the DLL values are emulated instruction by instruction), that `$I10_OUTPUT`'s 80 bit power-of-ten table equals the exact expansion for doubles outside the samples, strtod rounding of subnormals and of inputs beyond 17 digits. The string hash and the hashes of every userdata (lua_newuserdata blocks and lua_pushusertag records alike) and table keys are host independent (32 bit hash arithmetic, creation serials; a pointer / tag pair is found by an equality scan), see the README Every math library function (sin, cos, tan, asin, acos, atan, atan2, exp, log, log10, pow, sqrt, fmod, floor, ceil, abs, frexp, ldexp) is the deterministic libm of lane DEP-2 (Common/DeterministicLibm.h: glibc's x86-64 FMA results with an integer fma, NaN / signed zero / infinity results explicit, the same bits on Linux and Windows, x86-64 with or without FMA and AArch64 (macOS not run); not MSVCR71's x87 results); string.format's own exponents and gcinfo() (64 bit sizes) are the host's | `LuaRuntime` reports it for every state it opens | `lua numerics: round24 is one rounding ...`, `lua numerics: PC24 arithmetic agrees with the hardware-verified NumericState ...`, `lua numerics: the VM uses the numeric mode of its state ...`, `lua numerics: tostring of a number is MSVCR71's printf ...`, `lua numerics: the number text is exact and independent of the FPU rounding mode and the locale ...`, `lua numerics: tonumber with a base is MSVCR71's 32 bit unsigned strtoul ...`, `lua numerics: %.16g is MSVCR71's two stage conversion ... pinned against values executed from the DLL's own instructions`, `lua numerics: what stays unresolved is reported ...`, `lua: every host-independent hash ...`, `lua: iteration order does not depend on the heap ...`, `lua: lua_pushusertag userdata are deterministic too: independent processes ...` (test_lua_runtime.cpp, with the probe process tests/lua_userdata_probe.cpp) | scripts that print or compare computed numbers beyond 24 bits, any script using the math library | trace the FPU control word while a handler runs (oracle on Windows); run the math library functions through the oracle |

The logic bindings call into systems that do not exist in this build: Object (team, side, template, model condition flags, emotion state, fear factor, chanting, enraged, delayed death), AI (fear command, rampage, allegiance, forbid commands), Player (upgrades), Upgrade center, special power store, weapon store, partition manager (broadcasts, ObjectCountNearbyEnemies), horde contain, Drawable (showSubObject of an object), audio, the map script engine (203 conditions, 600 actions) and the logic frame. The closest path itself is still the stand-in below. Read but not applied: the exit-production straight path (RW 0x666036 .. 0x66607C: a unit in state 7 whose AI + 0x3BA is set takes computeQuickPath; when one was made the flag is dropped and the machine's goal becomes the destination, RW 0x6654AD with CMD_FROM_AI). Applied in the port it moved production exits (measured: test_aipow1 a frame 22 for 23; test_play4_heroes_retail 2 shots, 150 lost and 2 members killed in an order that has none; test_fly1_giant_bird's hunt chose a goblin archer for the lair) and, through the goal overwrite alone, sent an idle Battle-stance unit 826.975 from its guard spot (test_play4_stances_retail, bound 365; without the overwrite that test passes). | S-178 | code | Skirmish lobby inferences and the unresolved random-start consumer | (A) RESOLVED BY LANE START-1 (the consumer is GameLogic/NewGame, see S-270; the text below is the contract it implements, with the order confirmed from the RotWK disassembly): the lobby hands over `colour`, `playerTemplate` and `startPos` of -1 for random; `PlayerList::applySkirmishSlots` takes named factions, explicit RGB and start indexes, `LiveGame` applies slots before its logic RNG exists, and only the abstract / recording `NewGameSink` exist. A future skirmish-start lane must resolve them in retail's order (RotWK game.dat 0x62DC3E, S-001 caveat; clean BFME2 1.06 agrees): `populateRandomStartPosition` (0x62D7EA; BFME2 0x64485C) FIRST, then `populateRandomSideAndColor` (0x62D44C; BFME2 0x6444BE); start positions reserve explicit valid ones, then occupied non-observer slots in ascending slot order (random vacant-start branch, team / distance placement with a fixed candidate and tie order), observers after, geometry through the numeric facade; then for each occupied slot in ascending order its faction BEFORE its colour: a random / invalid faction first discards `seed % 7` LogicRandom(0,1) draws (seed accessor 0x6D3204), builds the ordered candidate list for the chosen start and mode and draws LogicRandom(0,1000) % candidateCount with its validity / retry logic, a random colour draws an inclusive index 0..NumColors-1 and rejects occupied colours; explicit valid choices skip their draws; every discard / retry draw, the range reduction and the RNG state must be reproduced and pinned with RNG-call and state golden vectors across peers before a deterministic start is claimed (ZH's side-and-colour-before-start order is wrong here). An adapter must refuse a message whose `SkirmishGameInfo::unresolvedRandomChoices()` is not empty rather than substitute a preferred colour, a null faction or start 0. (B) Inferred lobby contents: the interim setup source scans `playertemplate.ini`, `multiplayer.ini`, `gamedata.ini` by name (LOGIC-1's store replaces it); the faction list is Random plus the store's PlayableSide (+0x151) and not IsObserver (+0x150) templates labelled by DisplayName (RotWK 0x844BD4: no StartingBuilding or side filter) while the mode / map / start and hero restrictions of that routine are not recovered; colours are all configured ones, `AvailableInWotR = No` ones dropped only in strategic mode (flag +0x7C, RotWK 0x8440C7; the human / occupied / observer predicates of that routine are reduced to "another slot holds it"); AI is offered on every map (the +0x11 flag is not in MapCache); the battle-honors stars of the map list are ported since lane OWNERFIX-3 (S-5041); the slot-1 AI is Easy; the Hero combo stays empty (the Handicap combo is RotWK's since lane OWNERFIX-2, S-5031); the the rule rows are RotWK's since lane RULES-1 (S-3600); the chat / clan tabs answer 0 and their `InitGadgets` are notes; the ImageComboBox is native BFME code (RotWK 0x7252B6 / 0x7254A7 / 0x4A1EA3) of which the port has the call pattern (20 x 20 image cells AptRandomColor / AptWhiteBox tinted, selected image drawn centred) but not the key and child handling; the map picture is drawn since lane UI-2 (S-1480); the drop-down button's resize is RotWK's since lane UI-2 (RW 0x725688); display names and descriptions fall back to the file name (`$` labels) or the label without a string-file reader; `_global.InGame` is set true by the WindowManager (extern `InGame` answering "1" is verified at RotWK 0x815EF3 / 0x8154A5, the writer of the global is not); the retail AI levels are Easy 2, Medium 3, Hard 4, Brutal 5 and the human 6 (0x8419DB, 0x841A1B, 0x8009A7) | notes `skirmish-inferences`, `skirmish-random-unresolved` (at every StartGame message), `unported-gadget-init`, `unported-component` | `stops S-178 / S-179: ...`, `retail lobby input: the acceptance click-through ...` (unresolved list and note) and `skirmish setup: startPressed ...` (test_apt_skirmish.cpp) | a deterministic skirmish start; exact lobby contents; map list stars; Hero / Handicap; rule rows | write and oracle-test the skirmish-start consumer in the logic; read the remaining MpGameSetup restrictions |
| S-179 | code | Skirmish profiles and start | The profile flow follows RotWK's AptSkirmish state machine (static disassembly, S-001 caveat): `OnInitialized` sets state 1 (0x9280DB), the screen update (0x928CDB) invokes the movie's `ShowMain` -> state 5 and, with no current profile, `ShowAddProfile` (which opens the popup and sends `OnNewProfileMenu`); the persona entry's text updates enable / disable the popup's Select button (`PopupSelectBttnEnable` / `PopupSelectBttnDisable`, system handler 0x929589: raw text, not a trimmed or duplicate test), `GEM_EDIT_DONE` (data2 0) and a profile-list double click run the accept action (0x9294B3: `CloseProfilePopup`, then add in state 2 or change in state 4); `StartGame` sets state 10 (0x9280F0) and the update validates (0x8431B8): ok -> `CloseMain`, state 11; refused -> `ButtonReset`, state 7; cancelling the new-profile page with no profile leaves the screen (0x927FDA). Not recovered or inferred: which command sets state 2 / 3 / 4 (taken from the page it opens), what the delete page's accept does besides its button, the state-11 follow-up (the game is posted when validation passes, not when the close animation ends), where the preferences (last map, colour, faction, slot list) are stored (lane MENU-1: the profile names and the current one are RotWK's Skirmish.ini, S-3504), `SuppressStrategicPromo` and the stats commands (stored nowhere / notes), whether StartGame needs a profile (it does not), and the double click's row (the list sends the message mid-update, so the clicked row is selected first). | S-1830 | code | Blocked unit's path patch (lane MOVE-3, FB-0012) | Ported from RW: the blocked-repath handler RW 0x6631BF (the point 10, 20, ... ahead along the path, RW 0x765F31, until the path has no node after the point's node or the unit's footprint there is free, RW 0x6EDFD7: passable, checkForMovement RW 0x6EB792 with flags 1 for a HERO else 0x10, and a zero footprint cost; a patch is spliced, the blocked frames and flag cleared and the blocker remembered only when one was found), patchPath (pathfinder vtable 0xC1B8D8 slot 0x18, RW 0x6F7938: the A* from the unit's cell to the point's cell or the cells of the next 19 raw nodes, the footprint cost RW 0x6ED46C per step: goals of units of at least the unit's path priority cost 4 / 8 or block when parked, positions block; 3000 new cell records at most; the patch keeps every cell), the splice RW 0x767A66 with the raw-chain closest segment RW 0x765598 (opt 0), and the footprint radius of RW 0x6ED071 for every caller (the port narrowed hordes and ships to radius 1, which only RW 0x6F9850's neighbour expansion and the line test RW 0x6EE12D do, S-1834). Lane BACKLOG5 ported the GROUND-CONNECTION case only of the layer-change step RW 0x6F6286 (BFME2 decomp PathfinderCheckChangeLayers.cpp Pathfinder::CheckChangeLayers, tier A, donor) and calls it from patchPath, internalFindPath (RW 0x6FDBDD), findSafePath and computePath's patch (RW 0x6FCD22): a closed cell connected to the ground names its own index on the ground, which is that closed cell, so nothing is opened and no search changes. NOT implemented: the lookup of a cell on a bridge layer (RW 0x5E2E9C with the connection), the waypoint link expansion (RW 0x68229C, 0x93443D, the hop list at pathfinder + 0x1C1CC) and the wall layers (connection 16 and up, RW 0x76550F: the port's connection field has four bits, PathfindCell::m_connectsToLayer, and cannot hold them); no layer but the ground holds cells and no cell carries a waypoint (S-161), so a patch or a search never crosses a layer or a portal. What RotWK's rules leave, as read: only the blocked unit patches (lower path priority, then lower id, RW 0x66D16E); the other walks on; horde members never collide (RW 0x66E233 returns for a horde member, RW 0x6939DF); a moving unit holds no position (RW 0x8E26B7), so a patch may lead through a moving enemy; the targets include raw nodes behind the unit. Measured (OpenBFME timings, 90 crossings of infantry hordes with move orders, test_move3_horde_spacing.cpp): deep enemy member pair-frames 4040 -> 3377, head-on runs with the horde objects closer than 60: 12 -> 3; after the IDLE-1 merge (update phases RW 0x851E97 / 0x490AC4, the hub's disabled-motion skip RW 0x874724) 3798 and 16. Lane MOVE-3 r2 read the member side: a horde member never reaches blockedBy (RW 0x66E233 returns for a horde member through RW 0x6939DF; the only collide interface with that entry is AIUpdate's, vtable RW 0xC10578), the member's own steps test the terrain only (RW 0x66C748 -> 0x6EA4E7 / 0x6F1B3E), its slot destination too (RW 0x6EA5B2), the hub's crowd search is unused (S-1832), PhysicsBehavior has no collide interface (S-782), HordeMemberCollide reacts in melee only (RW 0x8C0518), and RotWK's collision manager tests the horde objects' own shapes (a 30 x 45 box, S-780), which meet later than the port's bounding circles. So RotWK lets the members of crossing hostile hordes overlap: only the lower-priority horde object stops and patches its path. Not verified against a retail run. | `Pathfinder::stops()` line `S-1830 the layer-change step ... implements only its ground-connection case ...` (pinned whole) | `backlog5: checkChangeLayers (RW 0x6F6286) is only its ground-connection case ...`, `backlog5: the two withdrawn rules are named at runtime ...` (test_pathfind_move.cpp), `move3 retail: crossing sweep of hostile infantry hordes with move orders (FB-0012)`, `ai move: two enemy units crossing head on both arrive ...` | how much hostile hordes overlap when they cross | a retail capture of two crossing hordes (frame positions) |

| S-1923 | code | Windows crash dumps (WINCRASH-1) | Godot 4.7's Windows crash handler (the official MinGW export templates: msvcrt signal handlers + libbacktrace) symbolizes its own executable only, and only on the main thread (msvcrt keeps SIGSEGV / SIGILL / SIGFPE handlers per thread): an extension frame is `openbfme.windows.template_debug.x86_64.dll+<offset> - no debug info`, and a crash on any other thread writes no dump at all. An in-process symbolizer (GodotCrashReport, rounds 1-2) was removed in round 3: code running inside the fault could not be shown safe. Since win-crash-1 (Devin, ported in DEVPORT-6: Common/System/WindowsCrashDump.cpp) the game installs its own handler at start on Windows: a crash on any thread writes a local minidump (user://logs/minidumps, never uploaded) from a writer thread made before any fault (no symbolization in the crashing code); the dump still names the extension's frames by offset only. The PDB is PRIVATE: every Windows build writes it (CodeView line tables, prefix-mapped, home prefixes scrubbed), tools/release/archive_symbols.sh keeps it only in the builder's local symbol store (it refuses a git work tree or a release folder), and the package audit and the commit gate refuse a .pdb or MSF bytes anywhere; an offset is mapped there with `llvm-symbolizer --obj=<dll> --relative-address 0x<offset>` | `RELEASE [S-1923] ...` printed into every Windows session log at start (scripts/release/release.gd), naming the offset-only frames (and, before win-crash-1, the missing worker-thread dump) | `test_the_session_log_names_stop_s1923_on_windows`, `test_debug_symbols_never_ship` (tools/release/test_pdb_tools.py) | a tester's crash log naming extension functions and lines directly (a worker-thread crash now leaves a minidump) | an out-of-process crash reporter / watchdog: a small helper process started with the game that, when the game dies, writes a minidump of it and symbolizes it with the private PDB on the developer side (no code in the crashing process) |

Timer priority is LifetimeUpdate when it is not waiting, then TEMPORARILY_DEFECTED (0x3E) even with a zero module timer, then HIDDEN (0x10) with ShowPalantirTimer; progress uses unsigned `(end - now) / (end - start)` and is clamped. Model condition 300 selects an active enemy viewer's enemy-perspective disguise template, otherwise the first upgrade-eligible StealthUpdate template; the displayed template gates IsTrainable, original experience progress is preserved, and a successful disguise query displays rank 1. LifetimeUpdate stores the WaitForWakeUp byte explicitly: setLifetimeRange does not clear it, UPDATE_SLEEP_FOREVER does not wake it, and a finite interface wake clears it and restarts the data range. See `docs/re/rotwk/RW_0x92F778.md`. Focused recovery tests pass (3 cases, 56 assertions) and the bounded regression passes (34 cases, 849 assertions). The waiting byte is client-read-only and adds no state-hash words, but interface Lifetime activation can change scheduling/death timing; expected pinned vectors were not changed and final vector/hash verification remains separate. | no runtime stop (closed) | `hud5 recovered ...` and the bounded HUD/hero regression (test_hud5_palantir.cpp), `stop S-760 ...` (test_hud2_palantir.cpp) | none | none |

| S-230 | code | Simulation audit tooling | The type-aware AST check of `tools/sim/sim_audit.py` needs libclang Python bindings (Linux: `libclang==18.1.1`; Windows: LLVM >= 19 and compatible bindings, see `tools/sim/README.md`) and runs where the build's compiler runs. MSVC includes come from the developer prompt and compile commands, including CMake's SYSTEM `/external:I` paths for Lua. Missing or unsupported Windows LLVM is a named error, not a skip. Implicit float -> int conversions are recognised through libclang's UNEXPOSED_EXPR nodes, template bodies are flagged only when no operand is a concrete integer, and the staged Lua C sources (lvm.c, whose arithmetic is the PC24 emulation by design) are not scanned. Every libclang error fails the audit, so a source whose headers libclang cannot parse (GCC-only intrinsics) must avoid them (`NumericState.cpp` reads MXCSR through compiler builtins). Without libclang the audit exits 2 (`--ast=skip`: exit 3, loud). | `tools/sim/sim_audit.py` (`STOPS`, printed on every run) | `tools/sim/test_sim_audit.py` (fixture TUs, configure of the real tree) | catching a bare float operation in a new simulation file before review | a CI image with libclang and the build's system headers |

| S-3710 | code | Relay log replay (ONLINE-2) | The online relay logs each player's own frame commands (FRAMEINFO, GAMECOMMAND, PLAYERLEAVE, DESTROYPLAYER, RUNAHEAD) once across its streams, and the players' MSG_LOGIC_CRC values travel inside them; `openbfme_peer --relay-log` assembles the frames as Network::relayCommands does. The replay it writes has a hash every CRC interval (not every frame) and no end record (the relay never sees the final state). ONLINE-3: the relay also logs the decider's DISCONNECTPLAYER (the agreed drop frame), the packet router's REJOIN and a REJOINED record per new incarnation, so drops and rejoins replay exactly; only a player whose frames end with neither a leave nor a logged drop (a crash at the end of the game) is taken as gone from its first frame without a FRAMEINFO | `RelayLog::stopLines()` `[S-3710]` (online peer reports, the `--relay-log` report, GameWorld.net_status of an online game); `RelayLog::Assembled::problems` names a drop or an incomplete frame | `online2: the lane's acceptance stops are reported (S-3710, S-3711)`, `online2: the relay log assembles into the frames the Network runs*` (test_online_relay.cpp); tools/net/online2_soak.sh (CRC parity of the replay from the log) | server-side replays of games with a disconnect drop; per-frame server replays | log the agreed drop frame (the relay can follow DISCONNECTPLAYER / DISCONNECTSCREENOFF), or the verifier of phase O4 |
| S-3711 | code | Relay trust and reach (ONLINE-2) | Phase O1's security model: tickets are HMAC-SHA256 under one key ring that the lobby and every relay share (a compromised relay could mint tickets; Ed25519 tickets would let relays only verify); the envelope is not encrypted: a bound slot moves only with the session token of its WELCOME (r2, Sol r1), but an on-path observer who reads that WELCOME could rebind the slot until the game ends; IPv4 only (NetAddress); no per-hop relay (docs/ONLINE.md phase O3; the reconnect half is ONLINE-3, S-3900). ONLINE-3: a rejoin ticket (version 2, a generation) binds a slot without its session token: whoever holds the lobby's newest rejoin ticket of a slot takes it (the lobby issues it only to the slot's signed-in player) | `RelayLog::stopLines()` `[S-3711]` (online peer reports, GameWorld.net_status of an online game) | `online2: the lane's acceptance stops are reported (S-3710, S-3711)`, `online2 r2 (Sol: player takeover)*` (test_online_relay.cpp) | a public service with untrusted relay operators or on-path attackers; IPv6-only players; rejoining a running online game | Ed25519 tickets (relays keep only the public key); an encrypted or MAC'd session (a key exchanged in HELLO / WELCOME); IPv6 sockets; phase O3 |
| S-380 | code | Apt calls without a function | a script call whose callee is not a function (a method a defined value lacks, any method of undefined / null, a named function no scope defines) calls nothing and returns undefined, as the retail interpreter does: RotWK CallMethod 0x00B1C220 / CallNamedMethodPop 0x00B1D3B0 (BFME2 0x00B08070 / 0x00B09200) resolve the member and skip a non-function silently, and nothing in them reaches the Apt log (the only ActionScript callers of the warning strings are the uncaught-exception reports RotWK 0x00B11574 / 0x00B1AB47). The player records the call as the note `call-without-function` (detail: the message and the clip the program runs on) instead of a script error; a name of the binary's native table this port does not implement (createTextField, removeTextField, loadVariables, getTextFormat: S-104; Object.registerClass: RotWK getter 0x00B22160, body 0x00AF1EB0, not read or ported, called by GuiTest and OnlineHome) stays a script error. An object or array value used as a callee (by method, by name or unnamed) is skipped the same way (RotWK 0x00B1AA5F pops the argc arguments and pushes undefined; an undefined receiver pops argc + 3 at 0x00B1C955 and pushes undefined; CallNamedMethodPop discards the result at 0x00B1D406); NewObject of a non-class stays a script error. The sibling order that makes InGameSideCommandBar's Button<i> call Button<i+1>.UpdateFrameState before it exists is the retail one (advance 0x00AF7030 -> frame controls 0x00B23540, frame actions 0x00B23850, sweep 0x00B0BC30; pool run 0x00AFA850; flush 0x00AF86A0) | note `call-without-function` | `player: siblings placed on one frame run their frame 0 actions in depth order*`, `stops S-380*` (test_apt_player.cpp, test_apt_interpreter.cpp: object / array callees and the operand counts), `retail shell walk*` (test_apt_shell_walk.cpp: every call of the shell walk pinned with its root cause), `retail MainMenu: with AptLevel0*`, the player corpus baseline | nothing in retail behaviour; a port gap that removes a function the movies expect now surfaces as a note, caught only by the pinned lists | none needed (retail behaviour); keep the pinned lists current |

The blocked test, the re-acquisition throttle (RW 0x74D46D) and the machine's transitions were already retail's. Now the crush run kills members with the CrushWeapon and the weapon strikes (measured engine pins of the test's scene: the Witch-king 4 swings of his mace and 3 CrushWeapon deliveries, 18 of 20 killed, 225 health in the run; the Mouth of Sauron 5 swings and 4 deliveries, 9 killed, 150 in the run; a swing takes 800 / 640 of a lone troll's 1500; with CRUSHER_LEVEL -1 on the hero there is no crush run and the horde blocks the Mouth of Sauron); every other hero of the PLAY-4 sweep is unchanged. The runtime note is removed. | S-3808 | code | map.ini overrides: every game starts from a freshly loaded world, a deliberate departure from RotWK (PLAY-4 found it, MAPRESET-1 decided by the owner, 2026-10-12) | Retail: a map's map.ini / solo.ini PlayerTemplate and skirmish AI blocks (SkirmishAIData, ArmyDefinition, AIBase, AIDozerAssignment, PlayerAIType) stay for the session: PlayerTemplateStore's reset (vtable 0xBF7CE8 slot 3) and TheSkirmishAIManager's (vtable 0xC13DFC slot 3) are the empty RW 0x63F3BF (the overrides go only at init, RW 0x5FEA89 -> Overridable::deleteOverrides RW 0x5E2E2E); GameLogic::reset drops TheGlobalData's alone (RW 0x62D3F7). So in retail a game after Helm's Deep or Minas Tirith places the factions' starting units at those maps' StartingUnitOffsets, and two peers (or a rejoining player in a fresh process) whose sessions played different maps start the same game with different data, which only the CRC notices. OpenBFME: `RetailObjectWorld::reload` discards the whole INI state (every store, registry, counter, name key, macro and parse record lives in the world's State) and runs the startup load again (warm: about 0.9 s on the Deck, 1.0 s on a CI host, measured with the test binary). A world a map.ini or a game changed is reloaded before the next map's map.ini (`applyMapIni`) and when its game ends (`endGame`, LiveGame's last member); skirmish, LAN / the peer, campaign, replays and a catch-up all load through LiveGame -> MapClassification::run -> applyMapIni. One game per world is live (`beginGame`: a second game's load fails, `applyMapIni` / `reload` throw "a game of this world is still live"); a test that runs two peers in one process gives the second its own world. Game setup (NewGame::prepareNewGame and the lobby limits in Godot, the peer and the skirmish menu) reads `setupPlayerTemplates()` / `setupCreateAHeroSystem()`, copies of the loaded PlayerTemplate store and CreateAHeroSystem kept outside the reloaded state (refreshed in place, so the shell's screens keep them across games). Every other reference into the stores is invalid after a reload: code must look templates up in the running game's world (tests that held one across games were changed). The engine identity (cmake/EngineId.cmake) hashes every engine source file, so replays and peers of earlier builds are refused by identity: no separate bump | `[S-3808]` in `RetailObjectWorld::acceptanceStops()` | the seven `mapreset1 ...` tests of test_mapreset1_retail.cpp (a world after 123 retail map.ini files, a test map.ini per block kind and a game with Create-a-Hero levels equals a fresh load on a probe of names, ids, counters and offsets; the live-game refusal and a viewer's map before a game; setup from the load's templates; the unloaded world; sequential games, two peers, the catch-up replay) | a retail-compatible game after a map with map.ini overrides differs from a retail client's session that played that map before | none planned (the owner's decision); cross-play with retail clients would need the session history matched |

Target facts: the SAS callbacks (RW 0x54F406 -> 0x54CF11 / 0x54CFD0 / 0x54D083) send both colours doubled when the map's multiplier exceeds 1 (RW 0x4AC97F, 0x54CF3B); defaultw3d.fxo lights classic materials per vertex (`saturate((sum(L.Color * N.L) * ColorDiffuse + AmbientLight * ColorAmbient + ColorEmissive) * vertex colour * 0.5)`, spec in oD1, pixel `tex * (oD0 + oD1) * 2`); the shaders compute it in gamma space and convert once (Godot lights and environment no longer touch W3D models). | S-3900 | code | Reconnect through the relay (ONLINE-3) | OpenBFME addition: RotWK 2.01 has no reconnect (a dropped player is gone: ConnectionManager::disconnectPlayer RW 0x8D57D8; the NetCommandType list RW 0x989449 has no rejoin). In an online game a player the disconnect path drops after its timeout (or at once when the relay reports its game restarted) is HELD: its army stays its own and idle for a rejoin window of OpenBFME's choosing (5 minutes), then the packet router hands it to an ally (retail's DESTROYPLAYER / MSG_SELF_DESTRUCT); a Kick vote hands over at once. Inference: a vote that completes while more than a tenth of the decider's bar remains is a kick, one at the end of the bar a timeout (the bar's automatic votes come at 0 %). A restarted client rejoins by re-simulating the relay's command log from frame 0 (no snapshots: the catch-up time grows with the game's length) and joins at the frame after the packet router's REJOIN; a rejoined slot is never packet router or drop decider again. The relay is the ping test of an online game (RW 0x8D7C78: a client that hears nothing from the relay blames its own link). LAN keeps retail's behaviour | `Network::onlineStopLines()` `[S-3900]` (every peer report; GameWorld.net_status of an online game) | `online3: the acceptance stops of the lane are reported (S-3900, S-3901)`, `online3: an online drop holds the player*`, `online3: rejoin:*`, `online3: the ping test through the relay*` (test_online3_rejoin.cpp); tools/net/online3_e2e.sh | rejoining long games quickly (60+ minutes: see docs/ONLINE.md); a rejoined player as packet router | snapshots (phase O4's deterministic save) to start the catch-up late; a per-player rejoin window in the lobby's START |
| S-3901 | code | Multiplayer pause (ONLINE-3) | OpenBFME addition: RotWK has no multiplayer pause: GameLogic::setGamePaused RW 0x625AF1 returns at once when isInMultiplayerGame RW 0x441B7C (game mode + 0x110 is 1 LAN or 5 internet) or RW 0x610A21 (+ 0x114 is 1 or 2) holds, the quit menu pauses only a game that is not multiplayer (RW 0x625AF1(1, 0, 1), AptQuitMenu.h), and RW 0x989449 lists no pause command. OpenBFME's pause: any player sends PAUSE (direct) naming its next execution frame, every peer runs the frames before it and stops; any player sends RESUME; no per-player limit; the screen shows "paused by" the player | `Network::onlineStopLines()` `[S-3901]` | `online3: pause and resume*` (test_online3_rejoin.cpp); tools/net/online3_e2e.sh (pause through the relay) | retail-compatible multiplayer (a retail client would not pause) | none needed for the enhanced profile; the retail-compatible profile disables it |
| S-3902 | code | Spectators (ONLINE-3) | Retail first: RotWK's observer is a lobby slot (PLAYERTEMPLATE_OBSERVER, -2) that takes part in the lockstep. Ported: with no slot of its own the spectator's local player is the ReplayObserver side, as retail's replay mode makes it (GameLogic RW 0x6282F0), whose map is permanently revealed (RW 0x62F91A), so shroud and fog are off; retail's PlayerList::observeNextPlayer (RW 0x6A8D2B, precondition RW 0x6A87F5, the observed player TheControlBar + 0x218, the shroud view player RW 0xB4F440) steps through the human players' views and back to every player's. Inference: Player + 0x5C == 0 is a human player. OpenBFME additions: an online spectator is no slot and no lockstep peer: the lobby's POST /v1/games/<id>/spectate gives a spectator ticket (version 3: the index 0..15, the delay) at the start or mid-game; the relay refuses every command record from it and sends it no STATUS; the spectator pulls the relay's log (LOG_REQUEST from frame 0, the catch-up path of S-3900) and follows it, comparing its CRCs with the players' (the relay's log file is the per-spectator buffer: nothing is queued per spectator, one silent for 30 s is dropped, nobody ever waits for one); the host's spectator delay (0..600 s) holds the log back by game frames (5 a second) behind the latest frame any player logged, and a finished game's log is released at that pace; the observed player's money, command points, rank and spell book points are a line on screen and Tab / Shift+Tab step (the Palantir's ObserverStuff buttons, SetObserverStuffState RW 0x8003F6, are not driven; retail's key binding for the command was not located) | `Network::onlineStopLines()` `[S-3902]` (every peer and spectator report; GameWorld.net_status) | `online3 spectators*` (test_online3_spectate.cpp: the ticket, the relay's refusal of a spectator's command, the delay, retail's ReplayObserver and observeNextPlayer); tools/net/online3_spectate.sh (home lab: start, mid, delay) | retail's observer slot in an online game (a retail client could not join as one); the Palantir's observer buttons | the observer UI of the Palantir (ObserverStuff) driven by the observed player; snapshots to start a late spectator's catch-up late (as S-3900) |

| S-4000 | code | Command-log saves (SAVE-2) | A save is the game's command log up to the saved frame F in OpenBFME's own container (`.obfmesave`: a header naming the build, engine id, profile identity, execution profile, game type, F and the state hash at F; the replay log; an empty snapshot part; each part with its SHA-256; Common/System/SaveGame/SaveGameFile.h). A load starts the log's game like a replay and plays the log to F at full speed with every recorded hash compared (GameClient/CommandLogLoad.h), checks the state hash at F, then hands the game to the player with a recording that continues the log. Owner decisions (2026-10-12): log-only first; a save loads only in the build that made it, refused with a message naming that build. Not built: retail's Xfer snapshot and versioned blocks (SAVE-4), multiplayer saves (SAVE-3); a load takes as long as the catch-up (ONLINE-3 measured 9 / 48 / 106 s for 10 / 30 / 60-minute games on the reference PC) | `CommandLogLoad::stopLines()` `[S-4000]` in `GameWorld.save_game` / `start_save_load` reports (`stops`) | `save2: the lane's acceptance stops are reported (S-4000 .. S-4005)` (test_save2.cpp), `save2 retail T1: ...` (test_save2_retail.cpp), `save2: a save loads only in the build that made it ...` (test_save2.cpp) | fast loads of long games; saves surviving an update | SAVE-4 (the Xfer framework and the native snapshot) |
| S-4001 | code | Cross-profile loads (SAVE-2, with UBER-1) | A save of the other execution profile (UBER-1's Faithful / Uber: the identity's `profile` line, the game setup's simulation profile) loads in single player only (owner decision 7): its log is replayed under the profiles it ran (its start message's, then its own profile switch records), so every recorded hash is compared, and at the saved frame GameLogic::setSimulationProfile switches to the player's profile; the switch is a record of the continued replay itself (replay format 6, `u8 5` frame / profile, which ReplayPlayback hands to the batch of that frame: FrameBatch::switchProfile), so the continued replay and every later save replay exactly too. INFERENCE: UBER-1 documents the profile as set before the first frame; nothing it keeps across frames was found that a mid-game switch breaks (its caches are rebuilt per frame). | S-4280 | code | The render thread's live-logic work when the logic is behind (RENDERWAIT-1) | NOT A PORT (retail RotWK runs its client after each logic step on one thread: RW GameLogic::update 0x62E4E8 has no parallel section). The render thread never waits for a logic frame: it reads and commands the live logic only in its render frame's client window (`LiveGame::beginClientWindow` at SceneTree process_frame, ended when `LiveGame::advance` has queued the frame's logic frames), and only while the worker is between frames. When the logic falls behind its clock the worker is inside a frame at most render frames, so the HUD's update, its queued input and movie presses, the audio, the effects' logic part, the tree events and the per-frame scripts' live reads run once per logic frame (the worker holds after each frame, at most 50 ms, for the next window), not every render frame: the camera, the drawables and the radar stay at the render rate, the HUD's reaction to input waits up to one logic frame. Measured on the Deck, 10k Uber battle: 65 of 491 render frames held the logic, the windows cost the worker 41.5 ms per logic frame (`UBER1 client windows`). INFERENCE, not measured against retail: what retail's client does while its logic step overruns (it cannot draw then at all). Review r2: (1) input issued in a window while older frames are queued waits as pending input and runs in the first frame queued after it (a driver game: pumped only with the worker idle, no batch acquired meanwhile), the frame the draining accessors gave; a creation (`LiveGame::createObject`) still drains the worker; other direct changes through the accessors in a window (the scripted harnesses' test hooks) act on the last completed frame, before the queued ones. (2) NOT BOUNDED while a window is open: the worker cannot start a frame until the render thread's work before `advance` is done (it reads the live logic); only the park before a window (50 ms) and the window's life (ended at the next render frame's start if no advance ended it, that frame gets none) are bounded. Measured longest window: 25.6 ms in the 10k Uber battle, 62.7 ms in a 4-player QA skirmish at 4x (the scripted player's work in the window) (`UBER1 client windows`, `GAME PERF WINDOWS ... longest window`). Left outside the window and still waiting when the worker is busy: the scripted harnesses' coroutine calls (qa_player.gd's clicks and probes, uber1_player.gd's census) and one-off reads (a campaign mission's teardown, a save) | `GAME PERF WINDOWS` (held / missed render frames, worker parks and held time, `GameWorld.get_client_window_stats`); the wait log names every remaining wait by site (`GAME PERF WAIT`, `UBER1 wait site`, `GameWorld.get_logic_wait_sites`) | `renderwait1 retail: 60 Hz render frames beside 250 ms logic frames never wait, get a client window every logic frame, and the logic is unchanged` (test_renderwait1.cpp: windows missed while the worker runs, at least one held window per logic frame, no wait, the single-threaded hash); `renderwait1 retail: the wait log names a blocking wait's site ...`; `renderwait1 retail: input issued in a window while frames are queued runs in the first frame queued after it ...` and `renderwait1 retail: a window no advance ended ...` (test_renderwait1.cpp); `renderwait1 r2 retail: lockstep: a HUD's orders issued in a client window with batches queued ...` (test_smooth_net.cpp); `test_per_frame_scripts_never_call_a_waiting_method_unguarded` (tools/perf3/test_renderwait1_per_frame.py) | a HUD that answers every render frame while the logic is behind (an RTS player's input during a big battle) | the HUD's selection, control bar and picking from the published snapshot (S-810's open part), then input outside the window |

| S-4530 | code | Adaptive run-ahead margin | Lane NETLAG-1: the lobby's run-ahead is 1 (RotWK's command delay: the packet router stamps a command with the current frame + 1, RW 0x8D4301 / 0x8D3AF1, BFME2 decomp ConnectionManager::sendLocalCommand / sendLocalCommandDirect, tier A), and Network::updateRunAhead's target (an OpenBFME choice, RotWK has no run-ahead command, S-720) is the one-way latency (half the planned round trip, and with three or more players the two longest of the router's planned round trips added: a link between two other players is at most their two links to the router, Sol r1 of NETLAG-2; lane NETLAG-2, the 80th percentile of the last 32 round-trip samples, Transport::plannedRoundTripMs; NETLAG-1 used the smoothed round trip plus four times its variation, which one late acknowledgement raised to 6 frames for 25 s in the owner's relay games of 2026-10-18) plus NetworkRunAheadSlack percent plus 25 ms, in whole 200 ms periods, + 1, decided at least once a second (RotWK's NetworkRunAheadMetricsTime of 5000 ms only slowed the way down) and lowered one frame per decision once the link has 50 ms more room than the lower value needs (on the staging relay 4 players at 150 ms one way flapped between 1 and 2 frames without it). The 25 ms is room for one frame's simulation and the phase between the peers' 5 Hz clocks (a peer sends its frame info for P + run-ahead - 1 when it completes P - 1, and the other peer runs that frame run-ahead - 1 periods after its next tick); NETLAG-1 kept half a period (about 90 ms one way kept 1), lane NETLAG-2 measured run-ahead 1 at 150 ms one way on the staging relay (2 to 3 waits of one frame in 80 s, 294 to 314 ms from a click to its effect against 488 to 499 ms at 2 frames); a one-way latency below about 159 ms keeps 1. OpenBFME beyond retail (lane NETLAG-2, the owner's choice of 2026-10-20): with early orders (NetworkConfig::earlyOrders, on by default; openbfme_peer / game.gd --no-early-orders) a player's order runs one frame before the run-ahead's frame while that frame is at least the planned one-way latency + 10 % + 40 ms away on the local 5 Hz clock, and that frame's FRAMEINFO is held until then (Network::setFrameDue); the staging relay measured 156 ms on a LAN and 178 to 247 ms through the relay at 0 to 75 ms against about 290 (tools/net/netlag2_table.py). MSG_LOGIC_CRC keeps the run-ahead's frame. MP-2's formula rounded the latency up to whole frames and added one, so every real link ran at 2 (400 to 600 ms from a click to its effect). Recordings made before the lane (run-ahead 2, another engine id) keep playing: ProfileIdentity::replayCompatible tolerates the `engine` line alone, for the ids in verifiedReplayEngines() (each has a fixture recorded by that build, engine/tests/data/netlag1), for replays only; live peers, lobby JOIN / START and saves compare the whole identity | `Network::stopLines()` `[S-4530]` (every peer report) | `netlag1 run-ahead: the lobby's run-ahead 1 is RotWK's command delay ...`, `netlag2 run-ahead: with RotWK's NetworkRunAheadMetricsTime of 5000 ms ...` (test_mp2_disconnect.cpp), `netlag2 planned round trip: ...` (test_netlag2.cpp), `netlag1 net retail: a 2-player LAN game on real UDP ...`, `netlag1 net retail: a 2-player online game through the relay process ...` (test_netlag1.cpp) | the command delay on links between about 159 and 270 ms one way (where retail's router player keeps one frame) | measure stalls against the margin on real internet links; or port the router (S-720) |

| S-4810 | code | Repeated disconnect frame (MP-DROP-1) | OpenBFME DIFFERENCE from RW 0x8D836B (processDisconnectFrame ignores an announcement at or below the sender's known disconnect frame): the same frame announced again counts once this peer's record of that announcement was closed: by the sender's DISCONNECTSCREENOFF of a frame at or after it, by this peer's release of it (the packet router with its screen off) or screen-off, or by the slot's rejoin. Why that is always a new announcement of the sender: a sender announces a frame again only after its notify state was reset (a new screen spell, turnOnScreen, or OpenBFME's repeat after a release, S-1121); each sender's stream is reliable, ordered and deduplicated (Transport) and nobody forwards another player's DISCONNECTFRAME; a stale DISCONNECTSCREENOFF (an earlier frame) does not close the record (ZH processDisconnectScreenOff). Why it is needed: here a screen goes off when the silence ends, not when a frame runs (RotWK's screen-off sender was not located, S-1121), so a moment of traffic from a silent slot (a restarted game's first datagrams before the relay's STATUS names it, a hung game that flickers back) turns a survivor's screen off and on at one frame; under retail's rule its second announcement was ignored, RW 0x8D861A's "every player on one frame" never held again and the game froze with every vote cast (ONLINE-5's S-4556); the packet router's release followed by the sender's repeat froze the same way. **Old clients:** the rule must run on the packet router (the decider); in a game whose router runs a client without it, a crash can still freeze the game: every player of an online game must update (no protocol change tells the versions apart). A due drop that waits logs why (`the drop of slot N waits: ...`) | `DisconnectManager::stopLines()` `[S-4810]` (every peer report, the Godot net_status stops); the disconnect log line | `mpdrop1: a crashed player whose silence is broken for a moment ...`, `mpdrop1 (Sol r1, S-4810 races) ...` (test_mpdrop1_disconnect.cpp: both fail under RW 0x8D836B's rule), `mp2 disconnect: the lane's acceptance stops are reported ...` (test_mp2_disconnect.cpp) | parity of the disconnect messages with retail's when a screen goes off without a frame; games mixing old and new clients | locate RotWK's DISCONNECTSCREENOFF sender (type 29) and its frame |
| S-4811 | code | Loading players (MP-DROP-1) | TARGET FACTS: RotWK's DisconnectManager runs while the others load, but its screen opens only once TheGameLogic + 0x120, the multiplayer load screen, is gone (RW 0x8D8B82); GameLogic's loading update (RW 0x62B385) keeps the load screen (its update, RW 0x62550D) while TheNetwork vslot 0xD0 (RW 0x65DA20: the manager's + 0x270, a slot is silent) is false and the logic frame is below 6, and deletes it (RW 0x625524) otherwise; the silence limit is four times NetworkDisconnectTime below frame 6 (RW 0x8D335D, RW 0xBFD298). GameLogic's testTimeOut (RW 0x625C92) has no caller and nothing sends NETCOMMANDTYPE_TIMEOUTSTART. Ported as that gate (DisconnectManager::loadScreenUp). So a loader that is heard keeps every player on the load screen however long it takes; a silent one takes them off it and is dropped by the bar. OpenBFME DIFFERENCE: its client sends nothing while it loads (RotWK's ConnectionManager handles NETCOMMANDTYPE_PROGRESS; INFERENCE from the donor ZH: a loading client sends its progress), so a player still loading four times NetworkDisconnectTime (60 s) after the first to finish is silent: the others leave the load screen and its bar (60 s) drops it (online: held for a rejoin). The Godot client draws its own loading screen (not this gate) | `DisconnectManager::stopLines()` `[S-4811]`; the disconnect log line `load screen gone at frame N` | `mpdrop1: a player whose game dies on the load screen ...`, `mpdrop1 (Sol r1): a slow loader that keeps sending is never dropped ...` (test_mpdrop1_disconnect.cpp); tools/net/online3_e2e.sh loaddeath | a player loading more than about two minutes after the others | send load progress (or keep-alives) from the loading client |
| S-4812 | code | Drop validation (MP-DROP-1) | OpenBFME's own (RotWK's processDisconnectPlayer, RW 0x8D89D7 tier A, disconnects the named slot with no check of its sender or frame): a DISCONNECTPLAYER is refused by name, before anything is queued or changed, unless its target is a player of the game other than its sender, its departed list names only players of the game other than the target and the sender (and this player only when it departed before: a rejoined player), its sender actually announced (a DISCONNECTFRAME; frame 0 counts, the zero a slot's record starts with does not) a disconnect frame at or after the drop frame to this player, and (another target) this player's own last announcement is that frame (RW 0x8D861A: a decider decides only when every in-game player it hears announced it); then lane MP-2's fallback-order authority. A refused drop is sent again by its decider when that player announces a frame at or before it. A held drop whose target was dropped meanwhile is discarded; one whose claimed departures name a player this peer still hears is refused after NetworkPlayerTimeoutTime. A drop of the receiver itself needs only its sender's announcement: a player whose outgoing link failed hears everyone and cannot tell it is the one gone, so a sender that announces a frame first can still drop the receiver (as any drop in RotWK); a drop of another player cannot be forged that way unless the receiver is itself waiting on that frame | `DisconnectManager::stopLines()` `[S-4812]`; the refusals in Network::errors() | `mpdrop1 hostile (Sol r1): a drop the whole decision does not support ...`, `mpdrop1 hostile: during a real crash, a drop from a player who is not the decider ...`, `mpdrop1: a survivor that had not announced the drop frame ...` (test_mpdrop1_disconnect.cpp) | parity with retail's acceptance of any drop | none needed for OpenBFME's protocol |

| S-4830 | code | Online count (lane ONLINECOUNT-1) | "Players online" is an estimate: accounts whose session token the lobby saw in the last 150 s (the online menu's own stats poll, a staging room's polls, any signed request) plus the human slots of started games whose relay log has not been uploaded; the lobby learns a game ended only from that upload, so a game whose log never comes counts until the lobby forgets it (3 h). | S-4860 | code | Performance mode join (UBERJOIN-1) | OpenBFME's own, no retail source (retail has one simulation): the online and LAN game lists tag an Uber game ("  - UBER" after the name; a Normal game has no tag), and Join on a game whose mode differs from the player's opens the game's own Yes / No box ("This game uses Uber performance mode. Switch to Uber mode and join? (Your setting changes back after the game.)", the reverse for Normal): Yes asks the host program to switch the simulation profile for that game and joins, No stays in the list, a refused switch is a named box. DECISION: the switch is restored (not kept) when the room is left, the join fails or is cancelled, or the game has ended (GameWorld::restore_simulation_profile, scripts/game.gd); Options.ini is never written. The lobby room record carries `mode` ("normal" / "uber"; absent = normal for old clients; any other value is bad_field; a join in the other mode is the 409 `mode` refusal); the LAN announcement carries the mode in the GameInfo's simulation profile (UBER-1's field, validated there). INFERENCE: the staging room shows its mode as a first chat line ("This room uses Uber performance mode.", Uber rooms only) because RotWK's staging movie has no mode label. The Options screen's Uber performance row is greyed inside an online or LAN room with the reason in its help line, and a change there is refused by name (a profile change that still got in is refused at START by the profile identity, as before). A LAN room is kept after its game (the players return to it): the switch stands until the room is left, and a main menu with no lobby screen leaves a held room and restores. The LAN announcement's profile field is optional where it ends the packet (absent = Normal; a present one is validated). NOT DONE: Quick Match (another lane) reads `OnlineLobbyClient::profile()` / online_status `mode`. | note `online-stops` "[S-4860] ..." when OnlineShell.apt is made (`AptOnlineShell::stopLines()`) | `uberjoin1: the lobby client reads and sends a room's performance mode`, `uberjoin1 retail: the server browser tags an Uber game ...` (test_online4_screens.cpp), `uberjoin1 retail: the LAN list tags an Uber game ...` (test_mp2_lan_lobby.cpp), `UBERJOIN-1: ...` (lobby.test.mjs) | a player who answers No cannot join a game in the other mode; a crash between the switch and the game's end leaves the process, not the setting, switched | none |

| S-5149 | closed | HoldGround and the FACE_OBJECT emotion (found with the slot centring, RW 0x86C4EA) | CLOSED (review of #73): the idle mood scan's reach was the port's, not RotWK's. A HoldGround horde's guard waits; when the enemy comes near, its TAUNT / POINT emotion enters the temporary FACE_OBJECT state (59, RW 0x74D68C / 0x75638B), whose update runs AIIdleState::update (RW 0x7553AB) and the mood scan (RW 0x66844A(1, 1)); a target it finds becomes a plain attack order (RW 0x66C536 -> aiDoCommand 0xB -> RW 0x66BD33: the machine cleared, state 10) that replaces the guard, as in RotWK (neither the idle update nor the scan reads the guard mode; the scan's AI + 0x34 test, RW 0x668489, is BeginTemporaryStateMachine's kept machine, RW 0x6630A9, not the guard; the FACE_OBJECT state is the machine's + 0x50, RW 0x74168E). What the port had wrong is the scan's reach: RotWK's range is RW 0x6FF412(obj, 3) (RW 0x668711 .. 0x668726), the vision range of RW 0x68E43B with the VISION attribute modifiers (HoldGround's FighterHordeStanceHoldGround: VISION -90%, a tenth) and the height factor, plus Object + 0xB8 (the bounding circle radius) and a computer unit's mood factor, and its search (RW 0x701443 -> 0x7006D7) measures the 3D centre distance (partition distance type 1, RW 0xA39090 / 0xA39340); the port read the template's VisionRange and measured to the target's edge, so the emotion's scan found the enemy formation beside the horde (811 from the spot in the test). With RotWK's reach (AIUpdateInterface::nextMoodTarget, AIGuardMachine::getAdjustedVisionRange, TargetFinder::CENTER_3D for the mood scan and the guard's scans, RW 0x70146A -> 0x7006D7) the test's HoldGround horde (range 31.5 + its bounding circle 54.1 = 85.6) waits until the enemy strikes it; the FACE_OBJECT scan then finds the attacker's centre at 72.5 and follows it when it flees (RotWK's chase when struck, confirmed in Ghidra by the triage of #73). A HoldGround horde whose scan does find a target follows it, as RotWK's (StopChaseDistance, RW 0x749FB2 .. 0x74A0DF, is not ported: S-325); after such an attack RotWK guards again in place (S-5148) | n/a (closed) | `play4 stances retail: a fleeing enemy is chased out of the guarded spot in Battle; in HoldGround the struck horde's FACE_OBJECT mood scan finds its attacker within a tenth of the vision and follows it; Aggressive follows it without a leash` (test_play4_stances_retail.cpp) | | |

| S-720 | code | Network run-ahead | RotWK paces a network game by a packet router: RW 0x65DD0F gates a frame on a 200 ms period from QueryPerformanceFrequency (RW 0x65DBFC; 0xD9F608 = 5) and drops lag beyond 2 s; RW 0x8D3FD9 (BFME2's hasPacketRouterFrameStall) stalls the router only when a peer's latest frame (ConnectionManager + 0x12060) plus the slack is at or below the current frame, the slack being 3 before frame 6 and then GlobalData + 0xC18 = NetworkRunAheadSlack (10 in 2.01's GameData.ini); non-router peers advance on announced frames and complete command counts (RW 0x8D4E89, 0x8D77B5); RW 0x8D4301 / 0x8D3AF1 (BFME2 decomp ConnectionManager::sendLocalCommand / sendLocalCommandDirect, tier A) stamp a command with the current frame + 1 on the packet router (a non-router peer sends its commands to the router, which stamps and relays them): the router's player waits one frame (200 to 400 ms from a click to its frame) on any link, a non-router player one frame plus its round trip to the router, and nobody waits for a slow peer until it is NetworkRunAheadSlack frames behind; RotWK has no RUNAHEAD / RUNAHEADMETRICS command (RW 0x989449). The router protocol is not ported: OpenBFME keeps MP-1's symmetric lockstep with RotWK's one frame on a LAN (lane NETLAG-1: the lobby's run-ahead 1, the adaptive target S-4530; MP-1 .. MP-3 used 2), and lane MP-2's packet router adapts the common command delay to its measured round trip (Network::updateRunAhead: every NetworkRunAheadMetricsTime ms, ZH ConnectionManager::updateRunAhead's (latency / 2) * fps plus NetworkRunAheadSlack percent, the latency being the transport's smoothed round trip plus four times its variation (the transport's resend timeout backs off per RFC 6298 so slow links get samples), + 1 frame, at least the lobby's run-ahead, down one frame per period; sent as ZH's RUNAHEAD frame command, OpenBFME type 0x82, which every peer applies when its frame runs; a command issued on protocol frame L executes at L plus the run-ahead the frames before L agreed on (Network::runAheadAt), so the MSG_LOGIC_CRCs of all peers keep one execution frame however far each logic worker has acquired batches; only the frame's packet router's RUNAHEAD (the role passes on at a drop frame) within the lobby's run-ahead .. maxRunAhead (25) is taken, a fall of more than one frame is refused) | `Network::stopLines()` `[S-720]` (every peer report) | `net core: the network layer's acceptance stops ...` (test_net_core.cpp), `mp2 run-ahead: the packet router's measured round trip sets every peer's command delay ...` (test_mp2_disconnect.cpp), `mp2 net retail: on a slow link ...` (test_mp2_net_retail.cpp), `netlag1 net retail: a 2-player LAN game on real UDP ...`, `netlag1 net retail: a 2-player online game through the relay process ...` (test_netlag1.cpp) | on a slow link every player waits alike (retail: only the non-router players) and a slow peer stalls everyone at once (retail: after 2 s); cross-play | port the router (lane NETLAG-1's estimate; the addresses this row named before, 0x8D0AA9 / 0x8D2F04 / 0x8D34B4, are BFME2's: RW 0x8D4E89 is BFME2 0x8D0AA9, BFMEConnectionManager::sendFrameInfo, and BFME2 0x8D34B4 lies in ConnectionManager::update, RW 0x8D77B5): the frame info publisher RW 0x8D4E89, the router's stamping and relay (RW 0x8D4301, doRelay RW 0x8D75AC), the non-router's frame ceiling and completeness (getFrameAdvanceCount RW 0x65DD0F, update RW 0x8D77B5) and the stall test RW 0x8D3FD9, all tier A / B with byte-matched decomp C++: an asymmetric frame model in Network, then every MP / ONLINE path re-proved (drop / router hand-over, rejoin from the relay log, spectators, pause, replays, the relay log's assembly): two to three lane rounds; best done with the retail packet layout, S-721) |

HUD-2 renders each dome alone with the device's W3D renderer in a SubViewport (SPHERE01 over black = A, SPHERE02 over white = M; one layer over a constant background survives the shader's linear conversion exactly) and a canvas shader with blending disabled computes clamp(D + A) * M from the screen behind the clip; the documented probe `tools/render/globe_probe.py` (OPENBFME_GLOBE_PROBE, windowed) finds every channel within one byte of that reference over black and over (0.25, 0.5, 0.75). | S-810 | code | Frame pacing: the logic worker's open limits | The logic runs on a worker thread (`LiveGame::Options::logicThread`, GameWorld option `logic_thread`, default on for `start_new_game`; off is the single-thread fallback), the one owner of the simulation while it runs a frame (frame / phase / object / module / command order and RNG unchanged). The logic tells its client through ordered data-only events (`GameClient/ClientEvents`, applied once in order; the drawables' client modules are resolved on the logic owner, `ModuleFactory::resolveClientModule`, so the render side never interns into the logic's name keys or counts in its factory), the drawables are posed from the immutable snapshot of the last completed frame (`GameClient/LogicSnapshot`). Review r2: no client code reads live objects while the worker runs a frame: the radar's blips (`Radar::blips(snapshot, ...)`) and the camera's lock / follow target (`TacticalCamera::setObjectSource`, `LiveGame::presentedObjectPosition`) come from the presented snapshot, the HUD's per-frame update is skipped and its input deferred (`InGameHudNode` replays it in order) in render frames the worker is busy in, and every bound query / command waits for the worker (`LiveGame::waitIdle`). Open: the HUD's selection, control bar and picking read the completed live state at those idle points instead of a snapshot. MP-1 integration (Sol, reviews r3 / r4, done): the frame driver (`LiveGameFrameDriver`) is split into a protocol-owner half (`pump`, `acquire`, `completed`) and a simulation-owner hook (`simulationAfterFrame`); the LockstepDriver, its Network, the Transport and the ReplayWriter have one owner, the thread that calls `LiveGame::advance`, which pumps the transport on every call (also `advance(0)`, while the worker runs and while the lockstep stalls), stamps the local input once against the protocol frame (the logic frame of the last completion consumed; `LockstepDriver::setInputSource` for input keyed to explicit protocol frames), acquires batch N only after the load barrier and once every needed slot's frame info and declared command count are in (zero-command frames included), each exactly once and in order, into a bounded FIFO (`kMaxQueuedBatches`, backpressure keeps the next batch with the driver). The worker checks N == the logic frame, installs the batch immediately before phase 1, runs the six phases once and publishes an immutable completion (frame N + 1, its hash, the CRC frame's breakdown, an RNG fold) with the snapshot; the protocol owner hands it to the driver in order (the replay's hash record N + 1, `Network::frameCompleted` from the captured breakdown, the CRC sent with execution frame N + 1 + run-ahead). `maxFramesPerAdvance` and a stall drop clock time only (`stalledFrames`), never a batch. `setFrameDriver` and `drainFrames` drain the queued complete batches (never a wait for the network) before a driver change, a leave, a replay's end or shutdown. The snapshot's local-player shroud read uses `ShroudManager::clientObjectStatus` (no cache write: `getObjectStatus`'s lazy status write is hashed and differed per peer). | `[S-810]` in `DrawableManager::Report::stops` (`RenderInterpolation::stopLines()`), carried by the `GameWorld` load report | `smooth1: the smooth-motion stops are reported ...` (test_smooth_interpolation.cpp), `smooth1 retail: the logic worker gives the single-thread game frame for frame ...` (test_smooth_threaded.cpp), `smooth1 retail: the render side's per-frame work ... runs beside the logic worker` (test_smooth_live.cpp; `tools/smooth/sanitize_threaded.sh tsan` for the race detector), `smooth1 r4 retail: lockstep batches on the logic worker ...` (test_smooth_net.cpp), `net retail smooth1: two peer processes with the logic worker ...` (test_net_retail.cpp, `tools/smooth/net_threaded.sh` under TSan / ASan) and the STOPS registration check (test_logic_objects.cpp) | HUD snapshot reads | snapshot fields for selection, health, production and picking |

The drawn pose itself is retail's (RW 0x6765B9: D3DXVec3CatmullRom over the previous, recorded, current and pending positions, RW 0xB27C80's slerp of the recorded towards the current transform, the stale snap when the last move is two frames old) from the snapshot RW 0x674B1F gathers in phase 1 (lane SMOOTH-2: the drawn position and the presented time go further, S-1140).
